and interchanging the nonnegative sums over $\ell$ and $\gamma_0$
gives
\[
 S_a\le
 \sum_{\gamma_0:\,a\in\gamma_0}u_{\gamma_0}
 \left[
  \sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac1{(\ell-1)!}
   \sum_{\gamma_2,\ldots,\gamma_\ell}
    |\varphi\left(\gamma_0,\gamma_2,\ldots,\gamma_\ell\right)|
    \prod_{i=2}^{\ell}u_{\gamma_i}
 \right].
\]
Applying \eqref{eq:core-pinned-majorant} to the bracketed sum and then
\eqref{eq:core-polymer-root} yields
\begin{equation}
 S_a\le\sum_{\gamma_0:\,a\in\gamma_0}u_{\gamma_0}e^{|\gamma_0|}
 =\sum_{\gamma_0\in\ensuremath{\mathsf{CS}}_\Psi(a)}
      q_{\gamma_0}e^{(1+\tau)|\gamma_0|}
 \le\rho.
 \label{eq:core-clause-root-bound}
\end{equation}
Since $a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$ was arbitrary, this proves
$\sum_{\Gamma:a\in U_\Gamma}|a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}\le\rho$
for every such $a$, as asserted in \eqref{eq:core-cluster-root}.


Finally, we show that the series $\sum_\Gamma a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma)$
converges absolutely and uniformly on $\Omega$.
Since every polymer is a nonempty subset of $\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$,
the definition of $U_\Gamma$ in \eqref{eq:core-cluster-data} gives
$\emptyset\ne U_\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$ for every tuple.
By \eqref{eq:core-clause-root-sum},
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}}S_a&=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}}
     \sum_{\Gamma:a\in U_\Gamma}
       |a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}}\sum_\Gamma
       \mathbbm{1}\left[a\in U_\Gamma\right]|a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}\\&=\sum_\Gamma
       \left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}}\mathbbm{1}\left[a\in U_\Gamma\right]\right)
       |a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}=\sum_\Gamma
       |U_\Gamma|\,|a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:core-clause-counting}
\end{equation}
Since \eqref{eq:core-polymer-root} implies $\tau\ge0$ and
\eqref{eq:core-cluster-data} gives $K_\Gamma\ge1$, we have
$e^{\tau K_\Gamma}\ge1$. Using $|U_\Gamma|\ge1$,
\eqref{eq:core-clause-counting}, and the bound $S_a\le\rho$ just
proved in \eqref{eq:core-clause-root-bound}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
 \sum_\Gamma|a_\Gamma|\le\sum_\Gamma|a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}\le\sum_\Gamma
       |U_\Gamma|\,|a_\Gamma|e^{\tau K_\Gamma}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}}S_a\le |\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}|\rho\le m\rho.
\end{align*}
The last inequality uses $\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}\subseteq\mathcal{C}$ and $|\mathcal{C}|=m$.
By \eqref{eq:core-cluster-data}, $|Y_\Gamma(\sigma)|\le1$ for every
$\sigma\in\Omega$. Consequently,
\[
 \sum_\Gamma\sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}|a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma)|
 \le\sum_\Gamma|a_\Gamma|\le m\rho<\infty.
\]
The Weierstrass $M$-test therefore gives absolute and uniform
convergence of $\sum_\Gamma a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma)$ on $\Omega$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:core-swap-comparison}
Let $s\ge1$ be an integer, let $\Omega_1,\ldots,\Omega_s$ be nonempty
finite sets, and put $\Omega=\prod_{i=1}^s\Omega_i$.
For each $i\in[s]$, let $\ell_i:\Omega_i\to(0,\infty)$ be a local
weight function. Let $\mathcal J$ be a finite set of labelled hyperedges
on vertex set $[s]$. Each $e\in\mathcal J$ has a nonempty vertex set
$V_e\subseteq[s]$; distinct hyperedges may have the same vertex set,
but are distinguished by their labels.
For each $e\in\mathcal J$, fix a real coefficient $a_e\in\mathbb R$
and an indicator function
$Y_e:\prod_{i\in V_e}\Omega_i\to\{0,1\}$.
Define the probability measure $\pi$ on $\Omega$ by
\begin{equation}
 \pi(\sigma)=\frac1{Z_\pi}
 \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
 \exp\left(\sum_{e\in\mathcal J}a_eY_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\right),
 \qquad \sigma\in\Omega,
 \label{eq:core-swap-measure}
\end{equation}
where $Z_\pi$ is the normalizing constant.
This measure is well defined and strictly positive. For $A\subseteq[s]$,
define
\begin{equation}
 R(A)=\sum_{\substack{e\in\mathcal J\\V_e\cap A\ne\emptyset}}|a_e|.
 \label{eq:core-swap-oscillation}
\end{equation}
Let $\zeta$ be a random labelled hypergraph on vertex set $[s]$, and write
$\zeta_e=\mathbbm{1}\left[e\in\zeta\right]$ for its edge indicators. These indicators are
independent and satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \forall e\in\mathcal J,\qquad
 \mathbb P_\zeta(\zeta_e=1)=1-e^{-2|a_e|}.
 \label{eq:core-zeta-law}
\end{equation}
For $j\in[s]$, let $\mathcal A_j$ be the vertex set of the connected
component of $\zeta$ containing $j$; in particular,
$\mathcal A_j=\{j\}$ when $j$ is isolated.
Then, for every $f:\Omega\to\mathbb R$,
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 \le\left(
   \max_{j\in[s]}\mathbb E_\zeta
   \left[2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_j\right)}\right]
   \right)\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^\pi(f).
 \label{eq:core-swap-tensorization}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem-pisigma-expansion}
For all $\sigma\in\Omega$, we have
$\pi(\sigma)\propto\left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
          \prod_{e\in\mathcal J}\bigl(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr)$.
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem-pisigmatau-expansion}
For each $\sigma\in\Omega$,
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{\eta\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}}
 \widehat\pi(\sigma,\eta)=\pi(\sigma).
 \label{eq:core-positive-lift-exact-marginal}
\end{equation}  
Moreover, for each $\sigma\in\Omega$ and $\eta\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}$,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 \widehat\pi\left(\eta\mid X=\sigma\right)
 =\prod_{e:\eta_e=1}
   \frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
        {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
  \prod_{e:\eta_e=0}
   \frac{1}{1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}.
\end{aligned}
 \label{eq:core-positive-lift-conditional-factorization}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:core-random-component-flip}
For every
$C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)$, let $\varepsilon_C$ be an independent
fair bit. Let $X^\varepsilon$ be the configuration obtained by swapping
exactly those components $C$ for which $\varepsilon_C=1$, and let
$\varepsilon^C$ denote the vector obtained by flipping only the bit
indexed by $C$.
Then, for every $f:\Omega\to\mathbb R$,
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 \le\frac12\mathbb E\!\left[
   \sum_{C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)}\mathbb E_\varepsilon\!\left[
     \left(f\left(X^\varepsilon\right)
           -f\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)\right)^2\right]\right].
 \label{eq:core-component-forms}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
For each nonempty $C\subseteq[s]$, define
\begin{equation}
 \theta_C=\sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}
   \mathbb P\left(C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)\mid X=\sigma\right),
 \qquad
 \widetilde f_C(\sigma)
 =f(\sigma)-\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[f(X)\mid X_{-C}=\sigma_{-C}\right].
 \label{eq:core-centered-block-function}
\end{equation}
The probability defining $\theta_C$ conditions only on the first
configuration $X=\sigma$, not on $\eta$ or the second sample
$\left(X',\xi\right)$. The remaining randomness comes from $\eta$
with its conditional law given $X=\sigma$ and the independent second
sample $\left(X',\xi\right)$ with law $\widehat\pi$. 
For $C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)$, passing from $\varepsilon$ to
$\varepsilon^C$ flips only the swap bit for $C$ and leaves the bits
for all other components unchanged. Since the components are disjoint,
every coordinate outside $C$ still takes its value from the same
configuration, $X$ or $X'$. Thus, pointwise,
\[
 \left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)_{-C}
 =\left(X^\varepsilon\right)_{-C}.
\]
Consequently, 
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   f(X)\mid X_{-C}=\left(X^\varepsilon\right)_{-C}\right]=\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   f(X)\mid X_{-C}=\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)_{-C}\right].
 \end{aligned}
\]
Substituting into the definition of $\widetilde f_C$ in
\eqref{eq:core-centered-block-function} yields 
\[
 \widetilde f_C\left(X^\varepsilon\right)
 -\widetilde f_C\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)
 =f\left(X^\varepsilon\right)-f\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right).
\]
For a fixed sample pair with $C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)$, the uniform
law of $\varepsilon$ is invariant under
$\varepsilon\mapsto\varepsilon^C$. Thus
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &\quad\quad\mathbb E_\varepsilon\!\left[
   \left(f\left(X^\varepsilon\right)
         -f\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)\right)^2\right]=\mathbb E_\varepsilon\!\left[
   \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X^\varepsilon\right)
         -\widetilde f_C\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)\right)^2\right]\\
 &\quad\le2\mathbb E_\varepsilon\!\left[
   \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X^\varepsilon\right)\right)^2
   +\left(\widetilde f_C\left(X^{\varepsilon^C}\right)\right)^2\right]
 =4\mathbb E_\varepsilon\!\left[
   \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X^\varepsilon\right)\right)^2\right].
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:core-centered-swap-bound}
\end{equation}
Hence, \eqref{eq:core-component-forms} gives
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 \le2\mathbb E\!\left[
   \sum_{C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)}\mathbb E_\varepsilon\!\left[
     \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X^\varepsilon\right)\right)^2\right]\right].
 \label{eq:core-centered-component-form}
\end{equation}


Conditional on $g$, the collection $\operatorname{Comp}(g)$ is fixed.
Fix $g$ and $\varepsilon$.
For each $\sigma\in\Omega$, by \eqref{eq:core-conditional-swapped-pair-law},
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb P\left(X^\varepsilon=\sigma\mid g,\varepsilon\right)
 &=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mathbb P\left(
   X^\varepsilon=\sigma,\left(X'\right)^\varepsilon=\omega
   \mid g,\varepsilon\right)=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}\mathbb P\left(
   X=\sigma,X'=\omega\mid g\right)\\
 &=\mathbb P\left(X=\sigma\mid g\right).
 \end{aligned}
\]
By the law of total probability conditional on $g$,
\begin{equation}
 \forall\sigma\in\Omega,\qquad
 \mathbb P\left(X^\varepsilon=\sigma\mid g\right)
 =\mathbb P\left(X=\sigma\mid g\right).
 \label{eq:core-conditional-swapped-marginal-law}
\end{equation}


Condition on $g$, so that $\operatorname{Comp}(g)$ is fixed.
By \eqref{eq:core-centered-component-form} and \eqref{eq:core-conditional-swapped-marginal-law},
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 \le2\mathbb E\!\left[
   \sum_{C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)}
     \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2\right].
 \label{eq:core-unswapped-component-form}
\end{equation}
Since the marginal law of $X$ is $\pi$, the law of total expectation gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb E\!\left[
   \sum_{C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)}
     \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2\right]
 =\sum_{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]}\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \mathbb P\left(C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)\mid X\right)
   \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2\right].
 \label{eq:core-component-conditioning}
\end{equation}
Substituting \eqref{eq:core-component-conditioning} into
\eqref{eq:core-unswapped-component-form} and using the definition of
$\theta_C$ in \eqref{eq:core-centered-block-function} yields
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 \le2\sum_{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]}
   \theta_C\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
     \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2\right].
 \label{eq:core-weighted-centered-bound}
\end{equation}
By the definition of $\widetilde f_C$ in
\eqref{eq:core-centered-block-function} and the definition of
conditional variance, for every nonempty $C\subseteq[s]$,
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2\mid X_{-C}\right]
 &=\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \left(f(X)-\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[f(X)\mid X_{-C}\right]\right)^2
   \mid X_{-C}\right]=\operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-C}\right).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:core-centered-block-second-moment}
\end{equation}
The law of total expectation, followed by
\eqref{eq:core-centered-block-second-moment}, gives
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2\right]
 &=\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
     \left(\widetilde f_C\left(X\right)\right)^2
     \mid X_{-C}\right]\right]=\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-C}\right)\right].
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:core-centered-block-averaged-variance}
\end{equation}
Substituting \eqref{eq:core-centered-block-averaged-variance} into
\eqref{eq:core-weighted-centered-bound} gives
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 \le2\sum_{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]}\theta_C\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-C}\right)\right].
 \label{eq:core-block-form}
\end{equation}

Fix a nonempty $C\subseteq[s]$ and an outside
configuration $y\in\prod_{i\notin C}\Omega_i$.
Let $\pi_C^y$ be the law of $X_C$ given $X_{-C}=y$.
For $x_C\in\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i$, let $f_y\left(x_C\right)$ denote
the value of $f$ on the unique assignment $\sigma\in\Omega$ satisfying
$\sigma_C=x_C$ and $\sigma_{-C}=y$.
We write $\left(x_C,y\right)$ for this assignment.
Define the product reference measure $\nu_C$ by
\begin{equation}
 \nu_C\left(x_C\right)
 \propto\prod_{i\in C}\ell_i\left(x_i\right).
 \label{eq:core-product-reference-measure}
\end{equation}
For each $x_C\in\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i$, define
\begin{equation}
 q_y\left(x_C\right)
 =\frac{\pi_C^y\left(x_C\right)}{\nu_C\left(x_C\right)},
 \qquad
 L_y=\min_{x_C}q_y\left(x_C\right)>0,
 \qquad U_y=\max_{x_C}q_y\left(x_C\right).
 \label{eq:core-density-definition}
\end{equation}
Since $\nu_C\left(x_C\right)$ is strictly positive, $q_y$ is
well defined.

By the definition of conditional probability,
\begin{equation}
 \pi_C^y\left(x_C\right)
 =\frac{\pi\left(\left(x_C,y\right)\right)}
        {\displaystyle\sum_{z_C\in\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i}
         \pi\left(\left(z_C,y\right)\right)}.
 \label{eq:core-conditional-probability-definition}
\end{equation}
Substitute \eqref{eq:core-swap-measure} into the numerator and denominator
of \eqref{eq:core-conditional-probability-definition}. The outside local
weight $\prod_{i\notin C}\ell_i\left(y_i\right)$ has the same value in
every term of the denominator and in the numerator, so it cancels from
their ratio. Similarly, if $V_e\cap C=\emptyset$, then
$Y_e\left(\left(z_C,y\right)_{V_e}\right)=Y_e\left(y_{V_e}\right)$ for
every $z_C$. Hence the corresponding exponential factor is also common
to the numerator and every term of the denominator and cancels. The
remaining factors are precisely those that may depend on $x_C$, giving
\begin{equation}
 \pi_C^y\left(x_C\right)
 \propto
   \left(\prod_{i\in C}\ell_i\left(x_i\right)\right)
   \exp\!\left(
     \sum_{\substack{e\in\mathcal J\\V_e\cap C\ne\emptyset}}a_e
     Y_e\left(\left(x_C,y\right)_{V_e}\right)
   \right).
 \label{eq:core-conditional-block-law}
\end{equation}
Here and below, the proportionality constants may depend on $C$ and $y$
but not on $x_C$.
Substituting \eqref{eq:core-conditional-block-law} and
\eqref{eq:core-product-reference-measure} into
\eqref{eq:core-density-definition} gives
\begin{equation}
 q_y\left(x_C\right)\propto
 \exp\!\left(
   \sum_{\substack{e\in\mathcal J\\V_e\cap C\ne\emptyset}}a_e
   Y_e\left(\left(x_C,y\right)_{V_e}\right)
 \right).
 \label{eq:core-explicit-density}
\end{equation}
Consequently, by \eqref{eq:core-explicit-density}, for any
$x_C,x'_C$,
\begin{align}
 \ln\frac{q_y\left(x_C\right)}{q_y\left(x'_C\right)}
 &=\sum_{\substack{e\in\mathcal J\\V_e\cap C\ne\emptyset}}a_e
   \left(Y_e\left(\left(x_C,y\right)_{V_e}\right)
         -Y_e\left(\left(x'_C,y\right)_{V_e}\right)\right)\le\sum_{\substack{e\in\mathcal J\\V_e\cap C\ne\emptyset}}|a_e|
 =R(C),
 \label{eq:core-log-density-ratio}
\end{align}
where the inequality uses $Y_e\in\{0,1\}$, and the final equality is
\eqref{eq:core-swap-oscillation}. Choose
\[
 x_C^{\max}\in\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{x_C}q_y\left(x_C\right),
 \qquad
 x_C^{\min}\in\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{x_C}q_y\left(x_C\right).
\]
Then \eqref{eq:core-density-definition} and
\eqref{eq:core-log-density-ratio} give
\begin{equation}
 \ln\frac{U_y}{L_y}
 =\ln\frac{q_y\left(x_C^{\max}\right)}
              {q_y\left(x_C^{\min}\right)}
 \le R(C).
 \end{equation}
 Hence,
 \begin{equation}
 \frac{U_y}{L_y}\le e^{R(C)}.
 \label{eq:core-density-ratio}
\end{equation}
Let $\lambda$ be a probability measure on
$\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i$, and let
$Z_C=\left(Z_i\right)_{i\in C}\sim\lambda$. For $j\in C$ and every
$z_{C\setminus\{j\}}$ having positive marginal probability, define the
conditional mean
\begin{align}
 m_{j,\lambda}\left(z_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right)
 &\triangleq\mathbb E_\lambda\!\left[
   f_y\left(Z_C\right)\mid
   Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}=z_{C\setminus\{j\}}
 \right]\notag\\
 &=\sum_{\substack{u_C\in\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i\\
                    u_{C\setminus\{j\}}=z_{C\setminus\{j\}}}}
   f_y\left(u_C\right)
   \mathbb P_\lambda\!\left(
    Z_C=u_C\mid
    Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}=z_{C\setminus\{j\}}
   \right).
 \label{eq:core-local-conditional-mean}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{align}
 \forall\,j\in C,\qquad
 V_j^\lambda\left(f_y\right)
 &\triangleq\mathbb E_\lambda\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\lambda\left(
    f_y\left(Z_C\right)\mid Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}
   \right)\right]\notag\\
 &=\sum_{z_C\in\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i}
   \mathbb P_\lambda\left(Z_C=z_C\right)
   \left(
    f_y\left(z_C\right)
    -m_{j,\lambda}\left(z_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right)
   \right)^2.
 \label{eq:core-local-conditional-variance-definition}
\end{align}
Equivalently, minimizing separately for each value of
$Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}$ gives the variational representation
\begin{equation}
 \forall\,j\in C,\qquad
 V_j^\lambda\left(f_y\right)
 =\inf_{\psi:\prod_{i\in C\setminus\{j\}}\Omega_i\to\mathbb R}
   \mathbb E_\lambda\!\left[
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)
          -\psi\left(Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right)\right)^2
   \right],
 \label{eq:core-local-conditional-variance-variational}
\end{equation}
whose minimizer is
$\psi=m_{j,\lambda}$ from \eqref{eq:core-local-conditional-mean}.

For every function $h$ on $\prod_{i\in C}\Omega_i$, the density
$q_y=\pi_C^y/\nu_C$ gives
\begin{align}
 \mathbb E_{\pi_C^y}\!\left[h\left(Z_C\right)\right]
 &=\sum_{z_C}\pi_C^y\left(z_C\right)h\left(z_C\right)=\sum_{z_C}\nu_C\left(z_C\right)q_y\left(z_C\right)
                  h\left(z_C\right)=\mathbb E_{\nu_C}\!\left[
     q_y\left(Z_C\right)h\left(Z_C\right)\right].
 \label{eq:core-local-change-of-measure}
\end{align}
Applying this identity with $h=(f_y-c)^2$, and then using the definition
of $U_y$ in \eqref{eq:core-density-definition}, we obtain
\begin{align}
 \operatorname{Var}_{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)
 &=\inf_{c\in\mathbb R}
   \mathbb E_{\pi_C^y}\!\left[
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)-c\right)^2\right]\notag\\
 &=\inf_{c\in\mathbb R}
   \mathbb E_{\nu_C}\!\left[
    q_y\left(Z_C\right)
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)-c\right)^2\right]\notag\\
 &\le\inf_{c\in\mathbb R}
   U_y\mathbb E_{\nu_C}\!\left[
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)-c\right)^2\right]\notag\\
 &=U_y\operatorname{Var}_{\nu_C}\left(f_y\right).
 \label{eq:core-upper-density-variance}
\end{align}

Combining \eqref{eq:core-local-conditional-variance-variational} with \eqref{eq:core-local-change-of-measure}, and the definition of
$L_y$ in \eqref{eq:core-density-definition}, we obtain
\begin{align}
 \forall\,j\in C,\qquad
 V_j^{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)
 &=\inf_{\psi}
   \mathbb E_{\pi_C^y}\!\left[
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)
          -\psi\left(Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right)\right)^2
   \right]\notag\\
 &=\inf_{\psi}
   \mathbb E_{\nu_C}\!\left[
    q_y\left(Z_C\right)
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)
          -\psi\left(Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right)\right)^2
   \right]\notag\\
 &\ge\inf_{\psi}
   L_y\mathbb E_{\nu_C}\!\left[
    \left(f_y\left(Z_C\right)
          -\psi\left(Z_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right)\right)^2
   \right]\notag\\
 &=L_y V_j^{\nu_C}\left(f_y\right),
 \label{eq:core-lower-density-variance}
\end{align}
where each infimum is over
$\psi:\prod_{i\in C\setminus\{j\}}\Omega_i\to\mathbb R$.
By \eqref{eq:core-product-reference-measure}, the coordinates under
$\nu_C$ are independent.  Applying Lemma~\ref{lem:efron-stein} to
$f_y$ under this product measure gives
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_{\nu_C}\left(f_y\right)
 \le\sum_{j\in C}V_j^{\nu_C}\left(f_y\right).
 \label{eq:core-product-variance-tensorization}
\end{equation}
By
\eqref{eq:core-upper-density-variance},
\eqref{eq:core-product-variance-tensorization}, \eqref{eq:core-lower-density-variance} and \eqref{eq:core-density-ratio}, we have
\begin{align}
 \operatorname{Var}_{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)
 &\le U_y\operatorname{Var}_{\nu_C}\left(f_y\right)\le U_y\sum_{j\in C}V_j^{\nu_C}\left(f_y\right)\le\frac{U_y}{L_y}
       \sum_{j\in C}V_j^{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)\le e^{R(C)}\sum_{j\in C}V_j^{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right).
 \label{eq:core-local-density-tensorization}
\end{align}
For $j\in C$, the coordinates outside $j$ decompose as
\[
 X_{-j}=\left(X_{-C},X_{C\setminus\{j\}}\right).
\]
Hence, for every $y$ with
$\mathbb P_\pi(X_{-C}=y)>0$, the definition of
$V_j^{\pi_C^y}(f_y)$ gives
\[
 V_j^{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)
 =\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-j}\right)
   \mathrel{\Big|}X_{-C}=y
  \right].
\]
The law of total expectation therefore yields
\begin{equation}
 \forall\,j\in C,\qquad
 \sum_y\mathbb P_\pi\left(X_{-C}=y\right)
   V_j^{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)
 =\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-j}\right)
  \right]
 =V_j^\pi(f).
 \label{eq:core-local-global-conditional-variance}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{eq:core-local-density-tensorization} and 
\eqref{eq:core-local-global-conditional-variance},
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 &\quad \mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-C}\right)\right]
 =\sum_y\mathbb P_\pi\left(X_{-C}=y\right)
   \operatorname{Var}_{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)\\
   &\le e^{R(C)}\sum_{j\in C}
   \sum_y\mathbb P_\pi\left(X_{-C}=y\right)
   V_j^{\pi_C^y}\left(f_y\right)=e^{R(C)}\sum_{j\in C}V_j^\pi(f).
 \label{eq:core-local-variance-comparison}
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

To bound $\theta_C$ defined in
\eqref{eq:core-centered-block-function}, put $p_e=1-e^{-|a_e|}$.
By \eqref{eq:core-positive-lift-conditional-factorization}, for every
$e\in\mathcal J$,
\begin{align}
 \mathbb P\left(\eta_e=1\mid X=\sigma\right)
 &\quad=\sum_{\eta_{-e}\in
       \{0,1\}^{\mathcal J\setminus\{e\}}}
   \widehat\pi\left(\eta_e=1,\eta_{-e}\mid X=\sigma\right)\notag\\
 &\quad=\frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
              {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
   \prod_{d\in\mathcal J\setminus\{e\}}
   \left(
    \frac{1}{1+w_dh_d\left(\sigma_{V_d}\right)}
    +\frac{w_dh_d\left(\sigma_{V_d}\right)}
           {1+w_dh_d\left(\sigma_{V_d}\right)}
   \right)\notag\\
 &\quad=\frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
              {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}.
 \label{eq:core-lift-single-edge-probability}
\end{align}
Since
$h_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\in\{0,1\}$, we have
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb P\left(\eta_e=1\mid X=\sigma\right) = \frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
      {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
 =\begin{cases}
   0,
      &h_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=0,\\[2mm]
   \displaystyle\frac{w_e}{1+w_e},
      &h_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=1.
  \end{cases}
 \label{eq:core-lift-single-edge-cases}
\end{equation}
Using $w_e=e^{|a_e|}-1$ from
\eqref{eq:core-positive-auxiliary-definitions} and
$p_e=1-e^{-|a_e|}$, we have
\begin{equation}
 \frac{w_e}{1+w_e}
 =\frac{e^{|a_e|}-1}{e^{|a_e|}}
 =1-e^{-|a_e|}=p_e.
 \label{eq:core-p-w-relation}
\end{equation}
Substitution into \eqref{eq:core-lift-single-edge-cases} therefore yields
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb P\left(\eta_e=1\mid X=\sigma\right)
 =p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right).
 \label{eq:core-lift-bernoulli-parameter}
\end{equation}
Likewise, for every $\omega\in\Omega$,
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb P\left(\xi_e=1\mid X'=\omega\right)
 =p_eh_e\left(\omega_{V_e}\right).
 \label{eq:core-second-lift-bernoulli-parameter}
\end{equation}


To compare $g$ with the random hypergraph $\zeta$ defined in
\eqref{eq:core-zeta-law}, we place all relevant random objects on a
common probability space. In this construction, $(X,\eta)$ and
$(X',\xi)$ are independent, each has distribution $\widehat\pi$, and
the auxiliary random hypergraph has the distribution of $\zeta$.


First draw $X,X'$ independently from $\pi$. For every label
$e\in\mathcal J$, introduce two auxiliary random variables $U_e,U'_e$,
each uniformly distributed on $[0,1]$. All $2|\mathcal J|$ variables
are mutually independent and are independent of $(X,X')$.
Define 
\begin{equation}
\forall e\in\mathcal J,\qquad \eta_e=\mathbbm{1}\left[U_e\le p_eh_e\left(X_{V_e}\right)\right],\qquad
 \xi_e=\mathbbm{1}\left[U'_e\le p_eh_e\left(X'_{V_e}\right)\right].
 \label{eq:core-lift-uniform-realization}
\end{equation}
Indeed, $0\le p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\le p_e<1$, so independence
of $U_e$ from $X$ and uniformity of $U_e$ give
\[
 \mathbb P\left(\eta_e=1\mid X=\sigma\right)
 =\mathbb P\left(U_e\le p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\right)
 =p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right).
\]
Thus, \eqref{eq:core-lift-bernoulli-parameter} is satisfied. Moreover,
conditional on $X=\sigma$, each quantity
$p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)$ in the comparison
$U_e\le p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)$ is deterministic. Since the
variables $(U_e)_{e\in\mathcal J}$ are independent, the bits
$(\eta_e)_{e\in\mathcal J}$ are therefore conditionally independent.
Consequently, for every
$\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}$,
\begin{align}
 \mathbb P\left(\eta=\varepsilon\mid X=\sigma\right)
 &=\prod_{e:\varepsilon_e=1}
      p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)
   \prod_{e:\varepsilon_e=0}
      \left(1-p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\right).
 \label{eq:core-uniform-lift-product-law}
\end{align}
Since $h_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\in\{0,1\}$, using
\eqref{eq:core-p-w-relation}, the two Bernoulli probabilities can be
rewritten as
\[
 p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)
 =\frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
        {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)},
 \qquad
 1-p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)
 =\frac{1}{1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}.
\]
Substituting these identities into
\eqref{eq:core-uniform-lift-product-law} gives
\[
 \mathbb P\left(\eta=\varepsilon\mid X=\sigma\right)
 =\prod_{e:\varepsilon_e=1}
   \frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
        {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
  \prod_{e:\varepsilon_e=0}
   \frac{1}{1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)},
\]
which is exactly the conditional distribution in
\eqref{eq:core-positive-lift-conditional-factorization}.

By \eqref{eq:core-positive-lift-exact-marginal}, the $X$-marginal of
$\widehat\pi$ is $\pi$. Therefore, the conditional probability formula
gives, for every $\sigma\in\Omega$ and
$\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}$,
\begin{equation}
 \widehat\pi(\sigma,\varepsilon)
 =\pi(\sigma)\widehat\pi\left(\varepsilon\mid X=\sigma\right).
 \label{eq:core-lift-joint-factorization}
\end{equation}
In our construction, $X\sim\pi$, and the conditional law of $\eta$ given
$X=\sigma$ is the same as the conditional law under $\widehat\pi$.
Consequently,
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb P\left(X=\sigma,\eta=\varepsilon\right)
 =\pi(\sigma)\widehat\pi\left(\varepsilon\mid X=\sigma\right)
 =\widehat\pi(\sigma,\varepsilon),
 \label{eq:core-coupled-lift-law}
\end{equation}
where the last equality is \eqref{eq:core-lift-joint-factorization}.
The same argument shows that $(X',\xi)\sim\widehat\pi$.
Moreover, $(X,(U_e)_{e\in\mathcal J})$ and
$(X',(U'_e)_{e\in\mathcal J})$ are independent, so $(X,\eta)$ and
$(X',\xi)$ are independent random pairs, each with joint distribution
$\widehat\pi$. Thus they have exactly the distribution assumed when
$g$ was defined. Define $g$ from these bits as before, with label $e$
open precisely when $\eta_e\vee\xi_e=1$.

Next, define
\begin{equation}
 \zeta_e=\mathbbm{1}\left[U_e\le p_e\ \text{or}\ U'_e\le p_e\right],
 \qquad e\in\mathcal J,
 \label{eq:core-dominating-edge-indicator}
\end{equation}
and let $\zeta$ be the labelled hypergraph that includes $e$ exactly
when $\zeta_e=1$. By independence and uniformity of $U_e,U'_e$,
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb P(\zeta_e=1)
 &=1-\mathbb P\left(U_e>p_e,\ U'_e>p_e\right)=1-(1-p_e)^2
 =1-e^{-2|a_e|}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:core-dominating-edge-probability}
\end{equation}
Different indicators $\zeta_e$ depend on disjoint pairs $(U_e,U'_e)$,
so they are mutually independent. They are also independent of
$(X,X')$, since their definitions use only the auxiliary variables.
Consequently, this $\zeta$ has exactly the independent-hypergraph law
specified in the lemma.

Recall $0\le p_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\le p_e<1$. By \eqref{eq:core-lift-uniform-realization} and \eqref{eq:core-dominating-edge-indicator},
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \eta_e=1
 &\ \Longrightarrow\ U_e\le p_eh_e\left(X_{V_e}\right)\le p_e
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \zeta_e=1,\\
 \xi_e=1
 &\ \Longrightarrow\ U'_e\le p_eh_e\left(X'_{V_e}\right)\le p_e
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \zeta_e=1.
 \end{aligned}
\]
It follows that the set of
labelled hyperedges of $g$ is a subset of the set of labelled hyperedges
of $\zeta$. 
Recall that $\mathcal A_j$ is the vertex set of the connected component
of $\zeta$ containing $j$.
Fix a nonempty deterministic set $C\subseteq[s]$ and a vertex $j\in C$.
On the event $\{C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)\}$, the set $C$ is the vertex
set of a connected component of $g$. Hence every vertex of $C$ is
connected to $j$ in $g$, and therefore also in $\zeta$. 
In other words, 
$C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)$ only if $C\subseteq\mathcal A_j$.
Consequently, for every $\sigma\in\Omega$,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb P\left(C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)\mid X=\sigma\right)
 \le\mathbb P\left(C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\mid X=\sigma\right)=\mathbb P_\zeta\left(C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\right),
 \end{aligned}
\]
where the equality uses independence of $\zeta$ from $X$.
Using the definition of $\theta_C$ in
\eqref{eq:core-centered-block-function}, for every $j\in C$,
\begin{align}
 \theta_C
 &=\sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}
   \mathbb P\left(C\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)\mid X=\sigma\right)
 \le\sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}
   \mathbb P_\zeta\left(C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\right)
 =\mathbb P_\zeta\left(C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\right).
 \label{eq:core-theta-component-bound}
\end{align}

Finally, substituting \eqref{eq:core-local-variance-comparison} into
\eqref{eq:core-block-form} gives
\begin{align}
 \operatorname{Var}_\pi(f)
 &\le 2\sum_{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]}
   \theta_C\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[
   \operatorname{Var}_\pi\left(f(X)\mid X_{-C}\right)\right]\le 2\sum_{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]}
   \theta_Ce^{R(C)}\sum_{j\in C}V_j^\pi(f)
 \notag\\
 &=2\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^\pi(f)
   \sum_{\substack{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]\\j\in C}}
   \theta_Ce^{R(C)}=\sum_{j=1}^s
   \left(
   2\sum_{\substack{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]\\j\in C}}
   \theta_Ce^{R(C)}\right)V_j^\pi(f).
 \label{eq:core-collected-variance-bound}
\end{align}
Fix $j\in[s]$. By \eqref{eq:core-theta-component-bound}, we have
\begin{align}
 &\quad 2\sum_{\substack{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]\\j\in C}}
   \theta_Ce^{R(C)}
\le 2\sum_{\substack{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]\\j\in C}}
   \mathbb P_\zeta\left(C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\right)e^{R(C)}
 =2\mathbb E_\zeta\!\left[
   \sum_{\substack{\emptyset\ne C\subseteq[s]\\j\in C}}
   \mathbbm{1}\left[C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\right]e^{R(C)}\right]
 \notag\\
 &=2\mathbb E_\zeta\!\left[
   \sum_{\substack{C\subseteq\mathcal A_j\\j\in C}}e^{R(C)}\right]
   \le2\mathbb E_\zeta\!\left[
   2^{|\mathcal A_j|-1}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_j\right)}\right]=\mathbb E_\zeta\!\left[
   2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_j\right)}\right]
 \le\max_{i\in[s]}\mathbb E_\zeta\!\left[
   2^{|\mathcal A_i|}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_i\right)}\right].
 \label{eq:core-coefficient-bound}
\end{align}
Indeed, for each realization of $\mathcal A_j$, exactly
$2^{|\mathcal A_j|-1}$ subsets of $\mathcal A_j$ contain $j$, and the
definition of $R$ in \eqref{eq:core-swap-oscillation} implies
$R(C)\le R(\mathcal A_j)$ whenever
$C\subseteq\mathcal A_j$.
Substitution into \eqref{eq:core-collected-variance-bound} proves
\eqref{eq:core-swap-tensorization}.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{lem-sizeajraj}
$\max_j\mathbb E_\zeta
       \left[2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_j\right)}\right]\le n$.
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- lemma-23: body_md -->
\label{lem-rqequalaj}
$\mathcal R_Q=\mathcal A_j$.
<!-- end lemma-23 -->
<!-- lemma-24: body_md -->
\label{lem:core-exploration-mgf}
$\mathbb E e^{T/2-2\rho Q}
 \leq 1$.
<!-- end lemma-24 -->
<!-- lemma-25: body_md -->
\label{lem:core-oscillation-incidence} 
For $A\subseteq[s]$, we have $R(A)\le\rho I(W_A)$.
<!-- end lemma-25 -->
<!-- lemma-26: body_md -->
\label{lem-wleksquareovertwodeltaiw}
$|W|\le \frac{k^2}{2\Delta}I(W)$.
<!-- end lemma-26 -->
<!-- lemma-27: body_md -->
\label{lem-winlq-wplust}
There is a connected variable set in $\mathcal{G}_\Phi$ containing $W$ and having
at most $|W|+T$ variables.
<!-- end lemma-27 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Recall that $a_t$ is the previously unused good clause selected at step
$t$ in the fixed deterministic order from those incident to the blocks
indexed by $\mathcal R_t$. For every $t<Q$, its choice gives
$\ensuremath{\operatorname{vbl}}(a_t)\cap W_{\mathcal R_t}\ne\emptyset$, because the exploration
rule selects $a_t$ only if it contains a variable from a block indexed
by $\mathcal R_t$, and
$W_{\mathcal R_t}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal R_t}B_i$ by
\eqref{eq:core-block-union}.
Since
$\mathcal R_t\subseteq\mathcal R_Q=\mathcal A_j$, we have
$W_{\mathcal R_t} \subseteq  W_{\mathcal A_j} = W$.
Hence, $a_t$ contains a variable from $W$.
By \eqref{eq-defpq} we have
\[
 \mathcal P_Q\subseteq
 \left\{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}:\ensuremath{\operatorname{vbl}}(a)\cap W\ne\emptyset\right\}.
\]
Consequently, by the definition of $I(W)$ in
\eqref{eq:incident-clause-count},
\begin{equation}
 Q=|\mathcal P_Q|
 \le\left|\left\{a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}:
       \ensuremath{\operatorname{vbl}}(a)\cap W\ne\emptyset\right\}\right|
 \le I(W).
 \label{eq:core-Q-IW}
\end{equation}
Moreover, by \Cref{lem-winlq-wplust},
there is a connected set $L$ in $\mathcal{G}_\Phi$ such that $W\subseteq L$ and $|L|\le |W|+T$.
By $W\subseteq L$ and \eqref{eq:incident-clause-count},
we have 
\[
 I(W) \leq I(L).
\]
By $L$ is a connected set in $\mathcal{G}_\Phi$, $|L|\le |W|+T$ and \eqref{eq:core-incidence-input},
we have 
\[
 I(L)
 \le C_{\mathrm{INC}}|L|+4\ln n
 \le C_{\mathrm{INC}}(|W|+T)+4\ln n.
\]
Hence,
\[
I(W)\le C_{\mathrm{INC}}(|W|+T)+4\ln n.
\]
By \Cref{lem-wleksquareovertwodeltaiw},
$|W|\le k^2I(W)/(2\Delta)$, so
\begin{align*}
 I(W)
 &\le C_{\mathrm{INC}}\left(\frac{k^2}{2\Delta}I(W)+T\right)
       +4\ln n.
\end{align*}
Moreover,
for $k\ge30$, the parameter definitions in
\eqref{eq:core-parameters} give
\begin{equation}
 \frac{C_{\mathrm{INC}}k^2}{2\Delta}
   =\frac{25}{3k^3}\le\frac1{100},
 \qquad
 C_{\mathrm{INC}}\rho
 =\frac{k^2\lambda}{25(6k^8\lambda+1)}
 \le\frac1{150k^6}\le\frac1{100},
 \qquad C_{\mathrm{INC}}\ge1.
 \label{eq:core-numerical-slack}
\end{equation}
Therefore,
\begin{align*}
 I(W)
 \le \frac{I(W)}{100}+C_{\mathrm{INC}}T+4\ln n.
\end{align*}
Rearranging yields
\[
 \frac{99}{100}I(W)\le C_{\mathrm{INC}}T+4\ln n,
\]
and hence
\begin{equation}
 I(W)
 \le\frac{100}{99}C_{\mathrm{INC}}T+\frac{400}{99}\ln n
 \le2C_{\mathrm{INC}}T+8\ln n.
 \label{eq:core-IW-bound}
\end{equation}

We now expand the pointwise estimate one step at a time. Since
the blocks $B_i$ are nonempty and pairwise
disjoint, by \eqref{eq:core-block-union} we have $|\mathcal A_j|\le |W_{\mathcal A_j}|$.
Thus, 
$W=W_{\mathcal A_j}$ implies  $|\mathcal A_j|\le |W|$.
Moreover, we have $R(\mathcal A_j)\le \rho I(W)$ by
\Cref{lem:core-oscillation-incidence}, and $Q\le I(W)$ by
\eqref{eq:core-Q-IW}. Therefore,
\begin{align*}
 \ln\!\left(2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R(\mathcal A_j)}\right)+2\rho Q
 &= (\ln 2)|\mathcal A_j|+R(\mathcal A_j)+2\rho Q \\
&\le (\ln 2)|W|+\rho I(W)+2\rho I(W)  \\
&\leq  |W|+3\rho I(W).
\end{align*}
By \Cref{lem-wleksquareovertwodeltaiw} and \eqref{eq:core-numerical-slack},
\begin{align*}
 \ln\!\left(2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R(\mathcal A_j)}\right)+2\rho Q  &\le \left(\frac{k^2}{2\Delta}+3\rho\right)I(W)
 \le \frac{4I(W)}{100C_{\mathrm{INC}}}.
\end{align*}
By \eqref{eq:core-IW-bound} and \eqref{eq:core-numerical-slack},
\begin{align}
 \ln\!\left(2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R(\mathcal A_j)}\right)+2\rho Q
 \le \frac{4}{100C_{\mathrm{INC}}}
       \bigl(2C_{\mathrm{INC}}T+8\ln n\bigr)
= \frac{2}{25}T+\frac{8}{25C_{\mathrm{INC}}}\ln n
\le \frac{T}{2}+\ln n.
 \label{eq:core-direct-charge}
\end{align}
Exponentiating \eqref{eq:core-direct-charge} and rearranging gives
\[
 2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R(\mathcal A_j)}
 \le n e^{T/2-2\rho Q}.
\]
Taking expectation and applying \Cref{lem:core-exploration-mgf} now yields,
\begin{equation}
 \max_j\mathbb E_\zeta
       \left[2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_j\right)}\right]\le n.
\end{equation}
The lemma is proved.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Assume $s\ge1$. 
Recall that a polymer $\gamma$ is a nonempty set of clause labels
$\gamma\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$ whose induced subgraph $\mathcal{H}_\Psi[\gamma]$
is connected. A cluster label $\Gamma$ is an ordered tuple
$\Gamma=(\gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_\ell)$ of polymers, with repetitions
allowed and finite length $\ell\ge1$.
Let $\mathfrak L$ be the set of all cluster labels, and define
\[
\mathfrak L_{\ge2}=\{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L:|J_\Gamma|\ge2\}.
\]
The clause-label set
$\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$ is finite, so it has only finitely many polymers (each
polymer is a nonempty subset of $\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$). Consequently, for each
fixed length $\ell$ there are only finitely many possible tuples
$(\gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_\ell)$. Taking the union over the positive
integer lengths shows that the set of all cluster labels, and hence its
subfamily $\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$, is countable. Choose an increasing
sequence of finite sets $\Lambda_N\subseteq\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$ such that
$\bigcup_N\Lambda_N=\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$.

We partition the terms of the expansion of $\ln Z(\sigma)$ in
\eqref{eq:core-cluster-root}, indexed by $\Gamma\in\mathfrak L$, according
to whether $J_\Gamma$ is empty, a singleton, or contains at least two
indices. The last class is indexed by $\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$.
Absolute convergence in Lemma~\ref{lem:core-cluster-expansion}
justifies this regrouping.
If $J_\Gamma=\emptyset$, none of the original clauses indexed by
$U_\Gamma$ contains a bad variable. Thus $\chi_a(\sigma)=1$ for every
$a\in U_\Gamma$, so $Y_\Gamma(\sigma)=1$ and
$a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma)=a_\Gamma$.
If $J_\Gamma=\{i\}$, we abbreviate $Y_\Gamma\left(\sigma_{J_\Gamma}\right)$
as $Y_\Gamma\left(\sigma_i\right)$ and define the local weight
\[
 \ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)=
 \exp\left(\sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L:J_\Gamma=\{i\}}
                      a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma\left(\sigma_i\right)\right).
\]
Absolute summability in Lemma~\ref{lem:core-cluster-expansion} ensures
that this exponent is finite, so $\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)>0$.
Exponentiating the regrouped expansion gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 Z(\sigma)
 &=\exp\left(\sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L:J_\Gamma=\emptyset}a_\Gamma\right)
   \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
   \exp\left(\sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}}
                    a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma\left(\sigma_{J_\Gamma}\right)\right).
 \end{aligned}
\]
The first exponential factor is independent of $\sigma$ and cancels
in the ratio $Z(\sigma)/\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}Z(\omega)$ in
\eqref{eq:core-extension-weight}. Hence
\begin{equation}
 \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)\propto
 \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
 \exp\left(\sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}}
                    a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma\left(\sigma_{J_\Gamma}\right)\right).
 \label{eq:core-interaction-representation}
\end{equation}
Define
\begin{equation}
 \pi_N(\sigma)=\frac1{Z_N}
 \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i(\sigma_i)\right)
 \exp\left(H_N(\sigma)\right),\qquad
 H_N(\sigma)=\sum_{\Gamma\in\Lambda_N}
      a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma_{J_\Gamma}),
 \label{eq:core-finite-interaction-measure}
\end{equation}
where $Z_N$ is the sum of the numerator over $\Omega$. Thus, for each
$N$, $\pi_N$ is a strictly positive probability measure on the same finite
state space $\Omega$.

For each $N$, apply the component-swap comparison
\Cref{lem:core-swap-comparison} to $\pi_N$ using the identification
\[
 \mathcal J=\Lambda_N,\qquad
 e=\Gamma,\qquad V_e=J_\Gamma,\qquad
 a_e=a_\Gamma,\qquad Y_e=Y_\Gamma.
\]
Under this identification, the oscillation in
\eqref{eq:core-swap-oscillation} is exactly the quantity $R(A)$ used in
\Cref{lem-sizeajraj}, restricted to labels in $\Lambda_N$. The edge
indicators have the same independent law
$\mathbb P(\zeta_\Gamma=1)=1-e^{-2|a_\Gamma|}$. Applying \Cref{lem-sizeajraj} to the finite label set $\Lambda_N$ gives
\begin{equation}
 \max_{j\in[s]}\mathbb E_\zeta\!\left[
   2^{|\mathcal A_j|}e^{R\left(\mathcal A_j\right)}\right]\le n.
\end{equation}
Applying \Cref{lem:core-swap-comparison} with this bound yields, for every $f:\Omega\to\mathbb R$,
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_{\pi_N}(f)
 \le n\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^{\pi_N}(f).
 \label{eq:core-finite-poincare}
\end{equation}

We now pass from the finite-truncation measures $\pi_N$, for which the
Poincar\'e inequality above follows from
\Cref{lem:core-swap-comparison} and \Cref{lem-sizeajraj}, to the target
measure $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}$. The convergence argument uses the absolute-summability
bound from \Cref{lem:core-cluster-expansion} and the interaction
representation \eqref{eq:core-interaction-representation}.
Define
\begin{align}\label{eq-defhsigma}
H(\sigma)\triangleq \sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}}
                 a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma_{J_\Gamma}). 
\end{align}
The proof of \Cref{lem:core-cluster-expansion} gives
$\sum_\Gamma|a_\Gamma|\le m\rho$. Since $|Y_\Gamma(\sigma_{J_\Gamma})|\le1$,
we therefore have
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}}
   \sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}
   \left|a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma_{J_\Gamma})\right|
 \le \sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}}|a_\Gamma|
 \le \sum_\Gamma|a_\Gamma|\le m\rho<\infty.
\end{equation}
Thus the series defining $H$ is absolutely convergent, and, because
$\Lambda_N\subseteq\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$,
\[
 H(\sigma)-H_N(\sigma)
 =\sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}\setminus\Lambda_N}
       a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma_{J_\Gamma}).
\]
Applying the triangle inequality and then
$|Y_\Gamma|\le1$ gives
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}|H_N(\sigma)-H(\sigma)|
 \le \sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}\setminus\Lambda_N}
       \sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}|a_\Gamma Y_\Gamma(\sigma_{J_\Gamma})|
 \le \sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}\setminus\Lambda_N}|a_\Gamma|.
\end{equation}
For any $\varepsilon>0$, absolute summability lets us choose a finite
$F\subseteq\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$ such that
$\sum_{\Gamma\in\mathfrak L_{\ge2}\setminus F}|a_\Gamma|<\varepsilon$.
Since $\Lambda_N$ is increasing and
$\bigcup_N\Lambda_N=\mathfrak L_{\ge2}$, there is $N_0$ such that
$F\subseteq\Lambda_N$ for all $N\ge N_0$. Hence, for such $N$,
\[
 \sup_{\sigma\in\Omega}|H_N(\sigma)-H(\sigma)|<\varepsilon,
\]
which proves $H_N\to H$ uniformly on $\Omega$.
Set
\[
\begin{aligned}
 W_N(\sigma)&\triangleq
   \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i(\sigma_i)\right)e^{H_N(\sigma)},
 &\qquad
 W(\sigma)&\triangleq
   \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i(\sigma_i)\right)e^{H(\sigma)},\\
 Z_N&=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}W_N(\omega),
 &\qquad
 \mathcal Z&\triangleq\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}W(\omega).
\end{aligned}
\]
The uniform convergence $H_N\to H$ implies, by continuity of the
exponential, that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-weight-convergence}
 \forall\sigma\in\Omega,\qquad W_N(\sigma)\longrightarrow W(\sigma).
\end{equation}
Because $\Omega$ is finite, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-partition-function-convergence}
\begin{split}
 |Z_N-\mathcal Z|
 &=\left|\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}
       \bigl(W_N(\omega)-W(\omega)\bigr)\right|\le\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}|W_N(\omega)-W(\omega)|\longrightarrow0.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Each $W(\omega)>0$ since $\ell_i(\omega_i)>0$ and $H(\omega)\in\mathbb R$;
as $\Omega\ne\emptyset$, it follows that
\[
 \mathcal Z=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}W(\omega)>0.
\]
By \eqref{eq:core-interaction-representation}, $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)$ is
proportional to $W(\sigma)$. Since $W$ is positive on the nonempty finite
set $\Omega$, normalizing this proportionality gives
\[
 \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)=\frac{W(\sigma)}{\mathcal Z}.
\]
Recall that $\pi_N$ was defined in \eqref{eq:core-finite-interaction-measure}
with weight $W_N$ and normalizer $Z_N$. By
\eqref{eq:core-weight-convergence} and
\eqref{eq:core-partition-function-convergence}, and since $\mathcal Z>0$,
we obtain
\[
 \forall\sigma\in\Omega,\qquad
 \pi_N(\sigma)=\frac{W_N(\sigma)}{Z_N}
 \longrightarrow
 \frac{W(\sigma)}{\mathcal Z}=\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma).
\]
For completeness, fix $j\in[s]$ and
$y\in\prod_{i\ne j}\Omega_i$. Since $\Omega_j\ne\emptyset$ and
$\Omega=\prod_{i=1}^s\Omega_i$, every pair $(x,y)$ with $x\in\Omega_j$
is a state in $\Omega$. By \eqref{eq:core-feasibility-extension},
$\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}$ assigns positive mass to each such state. Therefore
\[
 \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(X_{-j}=y)
 =\sum_{x\in\Omega_j}\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(X_j=x, X_{-j}=y)>0.
\]
The convergence above then implies
\[
 \forall \ x\in\Omega_j,\qquad \pi_N(X_j=x\mid X_{-j}=y)
 \longrightarrow
 \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(X_j=x\mid X_{-j}=y).
\]
Set $\Omega_{-j}:=\prod_{i\ne j}\Omega_i$. For
$\pi\in\{\pi_N,\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\}$, $x\in\Omega_j$, and
$y\in\Omega_{-j}$, define
\[
 p_j^\pi(x,y)\triangleq\pi(X_j=x,X_{-j}=y),\qquad
 p_{-j}^\pi(y)\triangleq\sum_{x\in\Omega_j}p_j^\pi(x,y),
\]
and let $f(x,y)$ denote $f$ evaluated at the configuration with
$j$th coordinate $x$ and remaining coordinates $y$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-vj-finite-sum}
 V_j^\pi(f)=\sum_{y\in\Omega_{-j}}\left[
   \sum_{x\in\Omega_j}p_j^\pi(x,y)f(x,y)^2
   -\frac{\left(\sum_{x\in\Omega_j}p_j^\pi(x,y)f(x,y)\right)^2}
          {p_{-j}^\pi(y)}
 \right].
\end{equation}
The pointwise convergence $\pi_N(\sigma)\to\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)$ and the
positivity established above give
\[
 p_j^{\pi_N}(x,y)\longrightarrow p_j^{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(x,y),\qquad
 p_{-j}^{\pi_N}(y)\longrightarrow p_{-j}^{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(y)>0
 \quad\text{for all }x\in\Omega_j,\ y\in\Omega_{-j}.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:core-vj-finite-sum} yields
\[
 V_j^{\pi_N}(f)\longrightarrow V_j^{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f).
\]
Similarly, for any probability measure $\pi$ on $\Omega$,
\[
 \operatorname{Var}_{\pi}(f)
 =\sum_{\sigma\in\Omega}\pi(\sigma)f(\sigma)^2
  -\left(\sum_{\sigma\in\Omega}\pi(\sigma)f(\sigma)\right)^2.
\]
Thus pointwise convergence of the masses also gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-variance-convergence}
 \operatorname{Var}_{\pi_N}(f)\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Var}_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f).
\end{equation}
Since $s$ is finite and each summand converges as shown above, we may
pass the limit through the sum:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-sum-variance-convergence}
\begin{split}
 \lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^{\pi_N}(f)
 &=\sum_{j=1}^s\lim_{N\to\infty}V_j^{\pi_N}(f)=\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Taking $N\to\infty$ in \eqref{eq:core-finite-poincare} and using
\eqref{eq:core-variance-convergence} and
\eqref{eq:core-sum-variance-convergence} yields:
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f)
 =\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{Var}_{\pi_N}(f)
 \le n\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^{\pi_N}(f)
 =n\sum_{j=1}^s V_j^{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f).
 \label{eq:core-limit-poincare}
\end{equation}

By the heat-bath Dirichlet-form identity \eqref{eq:core-dirichlet},
\[
 n\sum_{j=1}^sV_j^{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f)
 =sn\,\mathcal E_{\mathcal{K}}(f,f).
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:core-limit-poincare} gives
$\operatorname{Var}_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f)\le
sn\,\mathcal E_{\mathcal{K}}(f,f)$. If $|\Omega|>1$, the Rayleigh--Ritz
characterization in \Cref{sec:preliminary} then yields
\[
 \ensuremath{\mathsf{gap}}(\mathcal{K})=\inf_{\operatorname{Var}_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f)>0}
       \frac{\mathcal E_{\mathcal{K}}(f,f)}
            {\operatorname{Var}_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}(f)}
       \ge\frac1{sn}.
\]
Each of the $s$ blocks is a nonempty, pairwise disjoint subset of the
$n$ variables, so $s\le n$ and consequently $1/(sn)\ge n^{-2}$. If
$|\Omega|=1$, all functions on $\Omega$ are constant, so both sides of
the variance inequality \eqref{eq:core-limit-poincare} vanish. We use the
convention $\ensuremath{\mathsf{gap}}(\mathcal{K})=1$.

It remains to prove the total-variation estimate \eqref{eq:core-tv} when $|\Omega|>1$.
For each $j$, let $P_j$ be the conditional-resampling operator, with
transition kernel
\[
 P_j(\sigma,\omega)=\ensuremath{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\omega_{-j}=\sigma_{-j}\}}
 \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(X_j=\omega_j\mid X_{-j}=\sigma_{-j}),
 \qquad \sigma,\omega\in\Omega.
\]
View each $P_j$ as an operator on $L^2(\mu^{\mathrm{bad}})$, equipped with the
inner product $\langle f,g\rangle_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}=\mathbb E_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}[fg]$.
For any $g:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, its action is
\[
 (P_jg)(\sigma)=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}P_j(\sigma,\omega)g(\omega)
 =\mathbb E_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}[g(X)\mid X_{-j}=\sigma_{-j}].
\]
Thus $P_j$ is the orthogonal projection onto the subspace of functions
depending only on $X_{-j}$. In particular, $P_j=P_j^*=P_j^2$, where
$P_j^*$ is the adjoint with respect to this inner product; hence $P_j$
is positive semidefinite. The Markov operator corresponding to $\mathcal{K}$ is
$P=s^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^sP_j$, so it too is self-adjoint and positive
semidefinite. Since $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}$ has full support on the product space
$\Omega$, each block can be resampled to any of its values with positive
probability. Thus $\mathcal{K}$ is irreducible, and the eigenvalue $1$ is simple.
The gap bound in \eqref{eq:core-gap} then implies that every eigenvalue
of $P$ on the mean-zero subspace lies in $[0,1-1/(sn)]$. Consequently,
for every mean-zero $g$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-l2-contraction}
 \|P^tg\|_{L^2(\mu^{\mathrm{bad}})}
 \le\left(1-\frac1{sn}\right)^t
       \|g\|_{L^2(\mu^{\mathrm{bad}})}.
\end{equation}

Fix $\sigma_0\in\Omega$ and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-initial-density}
 g_0(\sigma)\triangleq\frac{\ensuremath{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\sigma_0\}}(\sigma)}{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)}-1.
\end{equation}
This is the initial density relative to $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}$, minus its constant part.
In particular, \eqref{eq:core-initial-density} gives
$\mathbb E_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}[g_0]=1-1=0$, so the contraction
\eqref{eq:core-l2-contraction} applies to $g_0$.
For $t\ge0$, write
$h_t(\omega)=\mathcal{K}^t(\sigma_0,\omega)/\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\omega)$ for the density
at time $t$. Since $\mathcal{K}^0$ is the identity kernel,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 h_0(\omega) - 1
 &=\frac{\mathcal{K}^0(\sigma_0,\omega)}{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\omega)} - 1
  =\frac{\ensuremath{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\omega=\sigma_0\}}}{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\omega)} - 1
  =\frac{\ensuremath{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\omega=\sigma_0\}}}{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)} - 1 = g_0(\omega).
\end{aligned}
\]
The last equality holds because the indicator vanishes
unless $\omega=\sigma_0$. Detailed balance gives
\[
 h_{t+1}(\omega)
 =\frac{1}{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\omega)}
   \sum_{\sigma\in\Omega}\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)h_t(\sigma)
        \mathcal{K}(\sigma,\omega)
 =\sum_{\sigma\in\Omega}\mathcal{K}(\omega,\sigma)h_t(\sigma)
 =(Ph_t)(\omega).
\]
Since $P$ is a Markov operator, it preserves the constant function $1$,
so $P1=1$. Subtracting this identity from the recurrence above gives
\[
 h_{t+1}-1=Ph_t-P1=P(h_t-1).
\]
Starting from $h_0-1=g_0$ and iterating this recurrence, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-density-evolution}
 h_t-1=P^t(h_0-1)=P^tg_0.
\end{equation}
By \eqref{eq:core-initial-density}, $g_0(\sigma_0)=1/\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)-1$, whereas
$g_0(\sigma)=-1$ for every $\sigma\ne\sigma_0$. Therefore,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-initial-density-norm}
\begin{aligned}
 \|g_0\|_2^2
 &=\sum_{\sigma\in\Omega}\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)g_0(\sigma)^2=\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)
   \left(\frac1{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)}-1\right)^2
   +\sum_{\sigma\ne\sigma_0}\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma)\\
 &=\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)
   \left(\frac1{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)}-1\right)^2
   +1-\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)=\frac1{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)}-1.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
By the total-variation identity \eqref{eq:def-TV-distance},
\[
 \ab\|\mathcal{K}^t(\sigma_0,\cdot) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
 =\frac12\mathbb E_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}[|h_t-1|].
\]
Using \eqref{eq:core-density-evolution}, this becomes
\[
 \ab\|\mathcal{K}^t(\sigma_0,\cdot) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
 =\frac12\mathbb E_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}[|P^tg_0|].
\]
Since $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}$ is a probability measure, Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\[
 \frac12\mathbb E_{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}}[|P^tg_0|]
 \le\frac12\|P^tg_0\|_2.
\]
Applying the contraction \eqref{eq:core-l2-contraction} (which applies to
$g_0$ because it is mean-zero by \eqref{eq:core-initial-density}), we get
\[
 \ab\|\mathcal{K}^t(\sigma_0,\cdot) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
 \le\frac12\|P^tg_0\|_2
 \le\frac12\left(1-\frac1{sn}\right)^t\|g_0\|_2.
\]
Finally, substituting the norm calculation \eqref{eq:core-initial-density-norm}
yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-tv-intermediate-bound}
 \ab\|\mathcal{K}^t(\sigma_0,\cdot) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
 \le\frac12\sqrt{\frac1{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)}-1}
       \left(1-\frac1{sn}\right)^t.
\end{equation}
By \eqref{eq:core-feasibility-extension}, every $\sigma_0$ has at least
one satisfying full extension. Since $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}$ is the induced marginal
described by \eqref{eq:core-extension-weight} and there are at most $2^n$
full assignments in total, it follows that
$\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)\ge2^{-n}$.
Consequently, the prefactor is bounded as follows:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-initial-density-prefactor}
 \sqrt{\frac1{\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\sigma_0)}-1}\le2^{n/2}.
\end{equation}
Moreover, we also have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:core-exponential-decay}
 \left(1-\frac1{sn}\right)^t
 \le\exp\left(-\frac{t}{sn}\right).
\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{eq:core-initial-density-prefactor} with
\eqref{eq:core-exponential-decay} and substituting both bounds into
\eqref{eq:core-tv-intermediate-bound} proves
\eqref{eq:core-tv}. If $|\Omega|=1$, the total-variation distance is
identically zero.
The theorem is proved.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:approximate-count-good-formula}
    Given $F \subseteq \mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{good}}$, $\varepsilon \in (0, 1/2)$ and $\delta \in (0, 1)$, there is an estimator $\widehat{Z}_{F}$ such that
    \begin{align*}
         
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{(1 - \varepsilon) Z_{F} \le \widehat{Z}_{F} \le (1 + \varepsilon) Z_{F}}{}{_{(1 - \varepsilon) Z_{F} \le \widehat{Z}_{F} \le (1 + \varepsilon) Z_{F}}}\left(#2\right)} 
 \ge 1 - \delta
    \end{align*}
    and its expected running cost is at most $P_1(I, \varepsilon^{-1}, \ln(1/\delta))$ where $P_1$ is a fixed polynomial.
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
If $F = \emptyset$, $Z_F = 1$ and we return $\widehat{Z}_{F} = 1$.
    Otherwise, order $F = \left\{a_1, \ldots, a_\ell\right\}$ and set
    \begin{align*}
        \forall j = 0, \ldots, \ell, \quad F_j \triangleq \left\{a_1, \ldots, a_j\right\}.
    \end{align*}
    Define
    \begin{align*}
        q_j \triangleq \Pr[\mu_{\Psi[F_{j - 1}]}]{a_j\; \text{is satisfied}}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore $Z_F = \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell} q_j$.
    By \Cref{lem:core-assignment-good-variable-extension}, $q_j \ge 1 - \mathrm{e} p_* \ge 1/2$.

    Set $h = \varepsilon/(16\ell)$ and
    \begin{align*}
        N_{\mathrm{sample}} \triangleq \left\lceil 128 \ell^2 \varepsilon^{-2} \ln \frac{2\ell}{\delta}\right\rceil.
    \end{align*}
    For each $j$, make $N_{\mathrm{sample}}$ independent calls to $\ensuremath{\mathsf{GoodSampler}}(F_{j - 1}, h)$ in \Cref{cor:good-fast-sampler} and let the returned assignments be $X_{j, 1}, \ldots, X_{j, N_{\mathrm{sample}}}$.
    Set
    \begin{align*}
        \forall t = 1, \ldots, N_{\mathrm{sample}}, \quad Y_{j, t} \triangleq \mathbbm{1}\left[X_{j, t}\; \text{satisfies}\; a_j\right].
    \end{align*}
    and $\widehat{q}_{j} \triangleq \sum_{t = 1}^{N_{\mathrm{sample}}} Y_{j, t}/N_{\mathrm{sample}}$.
    Finally output $\widehat{Z}_{F} = \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell} \widehat{q}_{j}$.

    We first bound the error of $\widehat{Z}_{F}$.
    For every $j = 1, \ldots, \ell$, put
    \begin{align*}
        \theta_j \triangleq  
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}\ifblank{\widehat{q}_{j}}{}{_{\widehat{q}_{j}}}\left[#2\right]} 
.
    \end{align*}
    By \Cref{cor:good-fast-sampler}, the law of the output of each call is within $h$ total variation distance of $\mu_{F_{j - 1}}$.
    Therefore $\ab|\theta_j - q_j| \le h$.
    The random variables $Y_{j, 1}, \ldots, Y_{j, N_{\mathrm{sample}}}$ are independent Bernoulli variables with mean $\theta_j$.
    By the Hoeffding's inequality,
    \begin{align*}
         
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{\ab|\widehat{q}_{j} - \theta_j| > h}{}{_{\ab|\widehat{q}_{j} - \theta_j| > h}}\left(#2\right)} 
 \le 2\mathrm{e}^{-2N_{\mathrm{sample}} h^2} \le \frac{\delta}{\ell}.
    \end{align*}
    Then by the union bound, with probability at least $1 - \delta$, for every $j = 1, \ldots, \ell$,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab|\widehat{q}_{j} - q_j| \le 2h = \frac{\varepsilon}{8\ell}.
    \end{align*}
    Using $q_j \ge 1/2$, we obtain that with probability at least $1 - \delta$,
    \begin{align} \label{eq:approximation-ratio-event}
        \forall j = 1, \ldots, \ell, \quad \ab|\widehat{q_j}/q_j - 1| \le \varepsilon/(4\ell).
    \end{align}
    On the event \eqref{eq:approximation-ratio-event},
    \begin{align*}
        \ab(1 - \frac{\varepsilon}{4\ell})^{\ell} \le \frac{\widehat{Z}_{F}}{Z_{F}} \le \ab(1 + \frac{\varepsilon}{4\ell})^{\ell}.
    \end{align*}
    For every $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$, $(1 - \varepsilon/(4\ell))^{\ell} \ge 1 - \varepsilon/4$ and $(1 + \varepsilon/(4\ell))^{\ell} \le \mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon/4} \le 1 + \varepsilon$.

    To complete the proof, we bound the expected cost of the estimation.
    There are $\ell N_{\mathrm{sample}}$ calls for $\ensuremath{\mathsf{GoodSampler}}(\cdot, \cdot)$.
    Then by \Cref{cor:good-fast-sampler}, $\ell \le \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I\right)$ and the choice of $N_{\mathrm{sample}} = \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(\ell, \varepsilon^{-1}, \ln(1/\delta)\right)$, the total expected cost of these calls is
    \begin{align*}
        \ell N_{\mathrm{sample}} \cdot P(I, h^{-1}) \le P_1(I, \varepsilon^{-1}, \ln(1/\delta))
    \end{align*}
    where $P$ and $P_1$ are fixed polynomials.
    Computing $\widehat{Z}_{F}$ takes time linear in $\ell$.
    Hence the expected running cost is at most $\ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I, \varepsilon^{-1}, \ln(1/\delta)\right)$ with a universal polynomial degree.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- lemma-28: body_md -->
\label{lem:bounded-approximate-update}
    For every component $B_i$, every feasible assignment $\sigma_{-i}$ on $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{bad}} \setminus B_i$ and $\eta \in (0, 1)$, one can implement an update whose law is within $\eta$ in total variation distance of $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\ab(\cdot \mid \sigma_{-i})$.
    Every returned core assignment is feasible and the update has a deterministic cost cap bounded by $P_2(I, \eta^{-1})$ for a fixed polynomial $P_2$.
<!-- end lemma-28 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Fix a component $B_i$ and a feasible assignment $\sigma_{-i}$ on $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{bad}} \setminus B_i$ and $\eta \in (0, 1)$.
    Since $\ab|B_i| < \log_2{n}$ by \Cref{lem:component-growth-size}, to generate $\Omega_i$, we list all assignments on $B_i$ and check the satisfiability by \Cref{lem:core-assignment-good-variable-extension} in time $2^{\ab|B_i|} \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I\right) = \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I\right)$.
    Hence $\Omega_i$ is enumerable in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I\right)$ and $\ab|\Omega_i| \le 2^{\ab|B_i|} < n$.
    For every $z \in \Omega_i$, $(z, \sigma_{-i})$ is feasible and set $w_z \triangleq Z_{D_{(z, \sigma_{-i})}}$.
    It is clear that $w_z > 0$ for every assignment $z \in \Omega_i$ and
    \begin{align*}
        p_z \triangleq \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\ab(z \mid \sigma_{-i}) = \frac{w_z}{W}
    \end{align*}
    where $W \triangleq \sum_{z \in \Omega_i} w_z$.

    Now we estimate $w_z$ for every $z \in \Omega_i$.
    Apply \Cref{prop:approximate-count-good-formula} on $D_{(z, \sigma_{-i})}$ with choice of $\varepsilon = \eta/8$ and $\delta = \eta/(4\ab|\Omega_i|)$.
    We obtain a non-negative estimate $\widehat{w}_{z}$ such that
    \begin{align*}
         
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{\ab(1 - \frac{\eta}{8})w_z \le \widehat{w}_{z} \le \ab(1 + \frac{\eta}{8}) w_z}{}{_{\ab(1 - \frac{\eta}{8})w_z \le \widehat{w}_{z} \le \ab(1 + \frac{\eta}{8}) w_z}}\left(#2\right)} 
 \ge 1 - \frac{\eta}{4\ab|\Omega_i|}.
    \end{align*}
    Let $\mathcal{E}$ be the event that every estimate satisfies the relative-error bound.
    By the union bound,
    \begin{align*}
         
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{}{_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}}\left(#2\right)} 
 \le \ab|\Omega_i| \delta = \frac{\eta}{4}.
    \end{align*}
    Set $\widehat{W} \triangleq \sum_{z \in \Omega_i} \widehat{w}_{z}$.
    Draw $\tau \in \Omega_i$ as follows:
    if $\widehat{W} = 0$, pick any feasible assignment in $\Omega_i$;
    otherwise, sample a candidate according to $\widehat{p}_{z} = \widehat{w}_{z}/\widehat{W}$.

    Now we bound the error.
    On $\mathcal{E}$, by \Cref{prop:approximate-count-good-formula}, for every $z \in \Omega_i$,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab|\frac{\widehat{w}_{z}}{w_z} - 1| \le \frac{\eta}{8}
    \end{align*}
    and
    \begin{align*}
        \ab|\frac{\widehat{W}}{W} - 1| = \ab|\sum_{z \in \Omega_i} p_z \ab(\frac{\widehat{w}_{z}}{w_z} - 1)| \le \frac{\eta}{8}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|\ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\tau \;\vert\; \mathcal E) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\cdot \;\vert\; \sigma_{-i})\|_{\mathrm{TV}} &= \frac{1}{2} \sum_{z \in \Omega_i} \ab|p_z - \widehat{p}_{z}| \\
        &\le \frac{1}{2(1 - \eta/8)} \sum_{z \in \Omega_i} p_z \cdot \frac{\eta}{4} \\
        &\le \frac{\eta}{4}.
    \end{align*}
    By the convexity of total variation distance,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|\ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\tau) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\cdot \;\vert\; \sigma_{-i})\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le  
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{\mathcal{E}}{}{_{\mathcal{E}}}\left(#2\right)} 
\frac{\eta}{4} +  
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{}{_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}}\left(#2\right)} 
 \le \frac{\eta}{2}.
    \end{align*}
    
    To bound the cost cap, \Cref{prop:approximate-count-good-formula} provides that the expect cost of each weight estimate is at most
    \begin{align*}
        P_1(I, 8/\eta, \ln(4\ab|\Omega_i|/\eta)) = P(I, \eta^{-1})
    \end{align*} 
    for some fixed polynomial $P$.
    Therefore, there exists $H_{I, \eta} = \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I, \eta^{-1}\right)$ with a universal polynomial degree such that the total running cost is at most $H_{I, \eta}$.
    Set the cap budget $C_{\eta} = \lceil 4H_{I, \eta}/\eta\rceil$.
    If the budget is exceeded, we stop the update and return any feasible assignment in $\Omega_i$.
    By the Markov inequality,
    \begin{align*}
         
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}\ifblank{\text{the budget is exceeded}}{}{_{\text{the budget is exceeded}}}\left(#2\right)} 
 \le \frac{H_{I, \eta}}{C_\eta} \le \frac{\eta}{4}.
    \end{align*}
    Then the capped output law is within $\eta/2 + \eta/4 < \eta$ in total variation distance with $\mu^{\mathrm{bad}}(\cdot \;\vert\; \sigma_{-i})$ and the deterministic cap is bounded by $\ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I, \eta^{-1}\right) \cdot (12/\eta) = P_2(I, \eta^{-1})$ for some fixed polynomial $P_2$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def:main-sampler}
    On input a $k$-SAT formula $\Phi$ with $n$ variables and $m$ clause, and a tolerance error $\varepsilon \in (0, 1/2)$, we define the $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FastSampler}}(\Phi, \varepsilon)$ as:
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item If $m = 0$, output $n$ independent fair bits.
        Otherwise, compute $\lambda$ and $\Delta$ as \eqref{eq:parameter-choice} and generate $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{high}}$, $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{bad}}$, $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{good}}$, bad components $B_1, \ldots, B_s$ and the good formula $\Psi$.
        If $s = 0$, call $\ensuremath{\mathsf{GoodSampler}}(\Phi, \varepsilon/8)$ and output the result.

        \item Check $\ab|B_i| < \log_2 n$ and construct $\Omega_i$ for every $i = 1, \ldots, s$.
        If there exists some $\Omega_i = \emptyset$, output $\bot$.

        \item Initialize any feasible assignment $\sigma_0 \in \prod_{i = 1}^s \Omega_i$.

        \item Set
        \begin{align*}
            T^{\mathrm{bad}} = n^4\ab(n + \ceil{\log_2(8/\varepsilon)} + 1), \quad \eta = \frac{\varepsilon}{8(T^{\mathrm{bad}} + 1)}.
        \end{align*}
        For $t = 1, \ldots, T^{\mathrm{bad}}$, simulate $T^{\mathrm{bad}}$ steps of $\mathcal{K}$: choose $i$ uniformly at random from the core components and use \Cref{lem:bounded-approximate-update} with error budget $\eta$ to update $B_i$ with the assignment on other components fixed.

        \item Let $\sigma$ be the assignment on $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{bad}}$ after $T^{\mathrm{bad}}$ updates.
        Call $\ensuremath{\mathsf{GoodSampler}}(D_{\sigma}, \varepsilon/8)$ once to obtain $\tau$ on $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{good}}$ and output the resulting pair $(\sigma, \tau)$.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:fast-sampler-analysis}
    On the structural event and the polymer-root estimate, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FastSampler}}(\Phi, \varepsilon)$ returns a satisfying assignment within $5\varepsilon/16$ in total variation distance with $\mu_{\Phi}$.
    Its expected running cost is at most $(I/\varepsilon)^{C_2}$ where $C_2$ is a universal constant.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
When $s = 0$, the proposition holds directly from \Cref{cor:good-fast-sampler}.
    Now we consider the case $s \ge 1$.

    We firstly provide the running cost.
    There are $T^{\mathrm{bad}} = \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(n, \ln{(1/\varepsilon)}\right)$ calls for the capped subroutine with accuracy $\eta = \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I, \varepsilon^{-1}\right)$ in \Cref{lem:bounded-approximate-update} to simulate an update for $\mathcal{K}$ and each simulator has a running time $(I/\varepsilon)^{C_1}$ for some universal constant $C_1 > 0$.
    For the final call $\ensuremath{\mathsf{GoodSampler}}(D_{\sigma}, \varepsilon/8)$, its expected cost is at most $\ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I, \varepsilon^{-1}\right)$.
    Therefore, the total expected running cost is at most $(I/\varepsilon)^{C_1} \cdot \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(n, \ln(1/\varepsilon)\right) + \ensuremath{\mathrm{poly}}\left(I, \varepsilon^{-1}\right) = (I/\varepsilon)^{C_2}$ for a universal constant $C_2 > 0$.

    Now we check the accuracy.
    By \Cref{thm:core-spectral-gap} with the choice of $T^{\mathrm{bad}}$,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|\mathcal{K}^{T^{\mathrm{bad}}}(\sigma_0, \cdot) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \frac{\varepsilon}{16}.
    \end{align*}
    Let $P_i$ be the exact update of the component $B_i$ and $\widehat{P}_{i}$ be its approximate implementation in \Cref{lem:bounded-approximate-update}.
    For every $\sigma \in \Omega_i$, by \Cref{lem:bounded-approximate-update},
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|P_i(\sigma, \cdot) - \widehat{P}_{i}(\sigma, \cdot)\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \eta.
    \end{align*}
    Then for the actual implementation core chain $\widehat{\mathcal{K}} = \frac{1}{s} \sum_{i = 1}^s \widehat{P}_{i}$, it holds that
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{\sigma \in \Omega}\; \ab\|\widehat{\mathcal{K}}(\sigma, \cdot) - \mathcal{K}(\sigma, \cdot)\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \eta.
    \end{align*}
    Let $\sigma_t$ be the actual assignment on $\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{bad}}$ after $t$ simulating updates.
    By the triangle inequality of the total variation distance, for every $t = 1, \ldots, T^{\mathrm{bad}}$,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|\mathcal{K}^{t}(\sigma_0, \cdot) - \ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\sigma_t)\|_{\mathrm{TV}} &\le \ab\|\mathcal{K}^{t - 1}(\sigma_0, \cdot) \widehat{\mathcal{K}} - \ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\sigma_{t - 1}) \widehat{\mathcal{K}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}} + \ab\|\mathcal{K}^{t - 1}(\sigma_0, \cdot) \widehat{\mathcal{K}} - \mathcal{K}^{t}(\sigma_0, \cdot)\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \\
        &\le \ab\|\mathcal{K}^{t - 1}(\sigma_0, \cdot) - \ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\sigma_{t - 1})\|_{\mathrm{TV}} + \eta.
    \end{align*}
    Then by induction,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|\ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\sigma_{T^{\mathrm{bad}}}) - \mathcal{K}^{T^{\mathrm{bad}}}(\sigma_0, \cdot)\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \eta T^{\mathrm{bad}} \le \frac{\varepsilon}{8}.
    \end{align*}
    By the triangle inequality of total variation distance,
    \begin{align*}
        \ab\|\ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\sigma_{T^{\mathrm{bad}}}) - \mu^{\mathrm{bad}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \frac{\varepsilon}{8} + \frac{\varepsilon}{16} \le \frac{3\varepsilon}{16}.
    \end{align*}
    Finally, given $\sigma = \sigma_{T^{\mathrm{bad}}}$, by \Cref{lem:core-assignment-good-variable-extension,cor:good-fast-sampler}, at least one feasible $\tau$ exists and $\ab\|\ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\tau) - \mu_{\Psi[D_\sigma]}\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \varepsilon/8$.
    Put everything together, and we obtain that $\ab\|\ensuremath{\mathsf{Law}}(\sigma, \tau) - \mu_\Phi\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le \varepsilon/8 + 3\varepsilon/16 = 5\varepsilon/16$, thus leading to the proposition.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
By \Cref{lem:expansion,lem:incident-clause-estimate,lem:high-degree-tail-bound} and \Cref{prop:approximate-count-good-formula} with choice of $\zeta = n^{-2}$, the structural event and the polymer-root estimate hold with probability at least $1 - o_n(1)$.
    Since the input length $I$ is upper bounded by $(n + nkm)^{C_1} \le (nk(\alpha + 1))^{C_1}$ for some universal constant $C_1 > 0$, by \Cref{prop:fast-sampler-analysis}, with probability at least $1 - o_n(1)$, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FastSampler}}(\Phi, \varepsilon)$ outputs a satisfying assignment of $\Phi$ within $\varepsilon$-total variation distance to $\mu_{\Phi}$ and its expected running time is at most
    \begin{align*}
        \ab(\frac{(nk(\alpha + 1))^{C_1}}{\varepsilon})^{C_2} \le \ab(\frac{nk(\alpha + 1)}{\varepsilon})^C
    \end{align*}
    for a universal constant $C > 0$.
    Thus we conclude the main theorem.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
For every $\sigma\in\Omega$, we have
\begin{equation}
 e^{a_eY_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
 =c_e\bigl(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr),
 \qquad
 c_e=\begin{cases}1,&a_e>0,\\ e^{a_e},&a_e<0.
       \end{cases}
 \label{eq:core-positive-factorization}
\end{equation}
To verify \eqref{eq:core-positive-factorization}, fix $e\in\mathcal J$
and $\sigma\in\Omega$. If $a_e>0$, then $c_e=1$,
$w_e=e^{a_e}-1$, and $h_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)$, so
\[
 c_e\bigl(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr)
 =1+\left(e^{a_e}-1\right)Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)
 =\begin{cases}
   1,&Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=0,\\
   e^{a_e},&Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=1.
  \end{cases}
\]
If $a_e<0$, then $c_e=e^{a_e}$, $w_e=e^{-a_e}-1$, and
$h_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=1-Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)$, so
\[
\begin{aligned}
 c_e\bigl(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr)
 &=e^{a_e}\bigl(1+\left(e^{-a_e}-1\right)\left(1-Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\right)\bigr)\\
 &=1+\left(e^{a_e}-1\right)Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)
 =\begin{cases}
   1,&Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=0,\\
   e^{a_e},&Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)=1.
  \end{cases}
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $Y_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\in\{0,1\}$, the displayed values equal
$e^{a_eY_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}$ in every case, proving
\eqref{eq:core-positive-factorization}.


By \eqref{eq:core-positive-factorization},
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\forall \sigma\in\Omega,\qquad \pi(\sigma)
 &\propto\left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
          \left(\prod_{e\in\mathcal J}c_e\right)
          \prod_{e\in\mathcal J}\bigl(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr)\\
 &\propto\left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
          \prod_{e\in\mathcal J}\bigl(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The second proportionality uses that $\prod_{e\in\mathcal J}c_e$ is
positive and independent of $\sigma$.
The lemma is proved.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Summing the full joint weight in \eqref{eq:core-positive-lift} over
$\eta$ gives, for every $\sigma\in\Omega$,
\begin{align}
 \sum_{\eta\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}}\widehat\pi(\sigma,\eta)
 &\propto
 \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
 \sum_{\eta\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}}
 \prod_{e:\eta_e=1}w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\notag\\
 &=\left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
 \prod_{e\in\mathcal J}
 \left(1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\right)\propto\pi(\sigma),
 \label{eq:core-positive-lift-marginal}
\end{align}
where the last proportionality follows from
\Cref{lem-pisigma-expansion}. The proportionality constants are
independent of $\sigma$; normalizing over $\sigma$ therefore shows that
the $\sigma$-marginal of $\widehat\pi$ is exactly $\pi$, namely,
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{\eta\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}}
 \widehat\pi(\sigma,\eta)=\pi(\sigma),
 \qquad \sigma\in\Omega.
\end{equation}
Moreover, for fixed $\sigma$,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 \widehat\pi\left(\eta\mid X=\sigma\right)
 &=\frac{
       \displaystyle
       \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
       \prod_{e:\eta_e=1}w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}{
       \displaystyle\sum_{\eta'\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}}
       \left(\prod_{i=1}^s\ell_i\left(\sigma_i\right)\right)
       \prod_{e:\eta'_e=1}w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}=\frac{
       \displaystyle\prod_{e:\eta_e=1}
       w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}{
       \displaystyle\sum_{\eta'\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}}
       \prod_{e:\eta'_e=1}w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}\\
 &=\prod_{e:\eta_e=1}
   \frac{w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
        {1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}
  \prod_{e:\eta_e=0}
   \frac{1}{1+w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The lemma is proved.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
For any fixed set $D\subseteq[s]$, define the involution $T_D$ on
$\left(\Omega\times\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J}\right)^2$ by
\begin{equation}
 T_D\bigl((\sigma,\eta),(\omega,\xi)\bigr)
 =\bigl(\left(\widetilde\sigma,\widetilde\eta\right),
        \left(\widetilde\omega,\widetilde\xi\right)\bigr),
 \label{eq:core-swap-map}
\end{equation}
where, for every $i\in[s]$ and $e\in\mathcal J$,
\[
 \left(\widetilde\sigma_i,\widetilde\omega_i\right)=
 \begin{cases}
  \left(\omega_i,\sigma_i\right),&i\in D,\\
  \left(\sigma_i,\omega_i\right),&i\notin D,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad
 \left(\widetilde\eta_e,\widetilde\xi_e\right)=
 \begin{cases}
  \left(\xi_e,\eta_e\right),&V_e\subseteq D,\\
  \left(\eta_e,\xi_e\right),&V_e\not\subseteq D.
 \end{cases}
\]
Whenever we apply $T_D$ below to an input pair
$\bigl((X,\eta),(X',\xi)\bigr)$, we assume that $D$ is a union of
connected components of its associated hypergraph $g$, where $e$ is
present exactly when $\eta_e\vee\xi_e=1$. 
Formally,
\begin{equation}
 D=\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal D}C\subseteq[s] \qquad \text{ for some }
\mathcal D\subseteq\operatorname{Comp}(g).
 \label{eq:core-swap-region}
\end{equation}
The subcollection $\mathcal D$ may be empty or equal to
$\operatorname{Comp}(g)$.

Fix an input pair $\bigl((X,\eta),(X',\xi)\bigr)$, and let $g$ be its associated hypergraph and $D$ be a union of components of $g$.
Fix $e\in\mathcal J$ with $\eta_e\vee\xi_e=1$. The hyperedge labelled
$e$ belongs to $g$, so any two vertices of $V_e$ are connected by this
hyperedge. Hence $V_e\subseteq C_e$ for a unique
$C_e\in\operatorname{Comp}(g)$. If $C_e\in\mathcal D$, then
\eqref{eq:core-swap-region} gives $V_e\subseteq C_e\subseteq D$.
If $C_e\notin\mathcal D$, then $C_e\cap C=\emptyset$ for every
$C\in\mathcal D$, because distinct connected components have disjoint
vertex sets. Thus \eqref{eq:core-swap-region} gives
\[
 C_e\cap D
 =C_e\cap\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal D}C
 =\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal D}\left(C_e\cap C\right)
 =\emptyset,
\]
and therefore $V_e\cap D=\emptyset$. We have proved
\begin{equation}
 \forall e\in\mathcal J,\qquad
 \eta_e\vee\xi_e=1
 \ \Longrightarrow\
 V_e\subseteq D\ \text{or}\ V_e\cap D=\emptyset.
 \label{eq:core-open-edge-region}
\end{equation}
Write
\[
 \left(\left(\widetilde X,\widetilde\eta\right),
       \left(\widetilde X',\widetilde\xi\right)\right)
 =T_D\bigl((X,\eta),\left(X',\xi\right)\bigr).
\]
For each $e\in\mathcal J$, define
\[
 F_e(\sigma,\eta,\omega,\xi)
 =\bigl(w_eh_e\left(\sigma_{V_e}\right)\bigr)^{\eta_e}
  \bigl(w_eh_e\left(\omega_{V_e}\right)\bigr)^{\xi_e},
 \qquad
 \sigma,\omega\in\Omega,\quad\eta,\xi\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal J},
\]
where a factor with exponent zero equals one, including $0^0=1$.
We verify the invariance of these factors in three cases.
\begin{enumerate}
 \item If $V_e\subseteq D$, the definition in
 \eqref{eq:core-swap-map} gives
 \[
 \left(\widetilde X_{V_e},\widetilde X'_{V_e},
  \widetilde\eta_e,\widetilde\xi_e\right)
 =\left(X'_{V_e},X_{V_e},\xi_e,\eta_e\right).
 \]
 Consequently,
 \[
 \begin{aligned}
 F_e\left(\widetilde X,\widetilde\eta,\widetilde X',\widetilde\xi\right)
 &=\left(w_eh_e\left(\widetilde X_{V_e}\right)\right)^{\widetilde\eta_e}
   \left(w_eh_e\left(\widetilde X'_{V_e}\right)\right)^{\widetilde\xi_e}\\
 &=\bigl(w_eh_e\left(X'_{V_e}\right)\bigr)^{\xi_e}
   \bigl(w_eh_e\left(X_{V_e}\right)\bigr)^{\eta_e}\\
 &=\bigl(w_eh_e\left(X_{V_e}\right)\bigr)^{\eta_e}
   \bigl(w_eh_e\left(X'_{V_e}\right)\bigr)^{\xi_e}
 =F_e\left(X,\eta,X',\xi\right).
 \end{aligned}
 \]
 \item If $V_e\cap D=\emptyset$, then $V_e\not\subseteq D$ because
 $V_e\ne\emptyset$. Hence \eqref{eq:core-swap-map} gives
 \[
 \left(\widetilde X_{V_e},\widetilde X'_{V_e},
  \widetilde\eta_e,\widetilde\xi_e\right)
 =\left(X_{V_e},X'_{V_e},\eta_e,\xi_e\right).
 \]
 Then $F_e\left(\widetilde X,\widetilde\eta,\widetilde X',\widetilde\xi\right)
 =F_e\left(X,\eta,X',\xi\right)$.
 \item If $V_e\cap D\ne\emptyset$ and $V_e\not\subseteq D$, then
 \eqref{eq:core-open-edge-region} implies $\eta_e\vee\xi_e=0$.
 Since $\eta_e,\xi_e\in\{0,1\}$, we have $\eta_e = \xi_e = 0$.
 Then \eqref{eq:core-swap-map} yields
 \[
 \widetilde\eta_e=\eta_e=0,\qquad
 \widetilde\xi_e=\xi_e=0.
 \]
 Therefore the zero-exponent convention gives
 \[
 F_e\left(\widetilde X,\widetilde\eta,\widetilde X',\widetilde\xi\right)
 =1=F_e\left(X,\eta,X',\xi\right).
 \]
\end{enumerate}
These cases exhaust all possibilities, so
\begin{equation}
 \forall e\in\mathcal J,\qquad
 F_e\left(\widetilde X,\widetilde\eta,\widetilde X',\widetilde\xi\right)
 =F_e\left(X,\eta,X',\xi\right).
 \label{eq:core-swap-edge-factor}
\end{equation}
For every $i\in[s]$, \eqref{eq:core-swap-map} also gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \ell_i\left(\widetilde X_i\right)\ell_i\left(\widetilde X'_i\right)
 &=\begin{cases}
   \ell_i\left(X'_i\right)\ell_i\left(X_i\right),&i\in D,\\
   \ell_i\left(X_i\right)\ell_i\left(X'_i\right),&i\notin D,
  \end{cases}\\
 &=\ell_i\left(X_i\right)\ell_i\left(X'_i\right),
 \end{aligned}
\]
Combined with \eqref{eq:core-swap-edge-factor} and \eqref{eq:core-positive-lift}, we obtain
\begin{equation}
 \widehat\pi\left(\widetilde X,\widetilde\eta\right)
 \widehat\pi\left(\widetilde X',\widetilde\xi\right)
 =\widehat\pi(X,\eta)\widehat\pi\left(X',\xi\right).
